Company Brief 
이차전지/석유화학 
Analyst 노우호 
wooho.rho@meritz.co.kr 
RA 황현식 
hyeonsig.hwang@meritz.co.kr Long Trading 구간  
3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후: ESS ↑, BEV ↓  
LG에너지솔루션의 3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후 배터리 섹터는 11/3 미국 중간선거
를 전후로 Trading Long 투자의견이 유효하다. 미국 중간선거 이후 특정정당 우세와 
별개로, 미국 Ex-China ESS 공급망 프리미엄은 유효하다. 동사 역시 그간의 ESS 
사업 난제(단위당 수율, 팩 병목)는 점진적 해소될 점이다. 반면 시장의 BEV 반등 기
대감에 동의하지 않는다. EU 프랑스와 독일의 내연기관 퇴출시점 지연 움직임 및 미
국 연비규제 정책 폐지 결정으로 전통 완성차 고객사들향 BEV 배터리 출하 부진은 
여전하겠다. 미국의 BEV 정책 변화시점은 28.11월 대선으로 지연될 전망이다.

ESS 모멘텀: 한국 제3차 ESS 중앙계약시장 사업자 선정 모멘텀 
대다수 시장 참여자들은 2026년 연말까지 LG에너지솔루션의 미국 ESS 60Gwh  규
모의 잔여 수주 달성 가시성에 집중한다. 해당 ESS 사업 수주 가이던스 관건은 시기
보다 방향성이다. 단, 당사가 주목하는 연말 모멘텀은 한국 정부향 제3차 ESS 중앙
계약시장 사업자 선정으로 판단한다. 3차 규모는 1.13Gw로 1&2차 계약 규모(합산 
1.13Gw) 대비 확대되었다. 또한 언론보도상 LG에너지솔루션은 해당 수주를 위한 핵
심 4대 소재 등 전 Value-chain 국산화를 선언한 바 있다. 해당 수주 확정시, 동사
를 비롯한 해당 Value-chain에 긍정적 모멘텀이 예상된다.

투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 51만원 제시   
26년 연중 지정학 Risk, 에너지 대전환 영향 등에 전력 인프라 수요는 꾸준할 전망
이다. 향후 국내 이차전지 산업은 ‘L’ Shaped Cycle 전망, 반면 동사는 스마트카 T사
향 원통형 전지 출하량 증가, ESS 제조업 이상으로 변모하는 과정으로 산업 평균 성
장률 Outperform 가능하다. 투자의견 Buy 유지, 적정주가는 추정치 상향에 51만원
을 제시한다.

Meritz Research  2026. 10. 15

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 510,000원 
현재주가 (10.14) 422,000원 
상승여력 20.9%

## Document 323: Downgrade of global equities to a Core allocation

Source PDF: all report pdf/Downgrade of global equities to a Core allocation.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Downgrade of global 
equities to a Core allocation  
Summary 
Event: We are downgrading global equities from Overweight 
to a Neutral/Core allocation. We continue to maintain a 
Neutral allocation to fixed income, Overweight on Gold and 
Underweight on Cash. There are more details in our Global 
Market Outlook publication on 30 October 2026. 
What does the equity downgrade mean?  
This downgrade implies a slightly lower allocation to global 
equities within a diversified portfolio. For our CIO Foundation 
Balanced portfolio, this would imply an equity allocation of 
52.5%, down from 53 .2%. We continue to expect positive 
returns for global equities  in the coming 6 -12 months , 
though equity investors may need to recalibrate expectations 
lower, compared with the 15 -20% annualised gains 
experienced in the last few years. Our capital market 
assumptions still suggest a mid -to-high single digit annual 
returns over the next few years, consistent with a transition 
to a mid -cycle bull market . Consensus estimates point to 
16% earnings growth for global equities in 2027, which 
should overcome various headwinds to deliver positive 
returns for equities over the next 12 months. Investors should 
thus, continue to maintain exposure to equities  within a 
diversified portfolio. 
In addition, we see a changing playbook  to prepare for 
higher volatility, more active management and greater 
diversification, as discussed below. 
What does it mean for regional equities 
positioning? 
Within a global equities portfolio, we remain Overweight US 
and Asia ex -Japan (AxJ) equities , which are expected to 
deliver the highest earnings growth in 2027 amongst the 
major regions. This growth is led by the technology sector, 
which carries the largest weight in both regions. Within the 
diversified equities portfolio, we maintain Core allocations to

53.2% for our CIO Foundation Balanced portfolio

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 30 October 2026

Euro area equities, which offer lower valuations, and Japan 
equities, which is backed by a reflating economy. We are 
Underweight UK equities, where the sector composition 
offers lower growth prospects in 2027. Within the AxJ region, 
we are Overweight Taiwan equities, with attractive structural 
growth driven by its semiconductor industry. We are also 
Overweight India equities, which offers diversified non-tech 
growth drivers. Taiwan and India equities both generate 
attractive returns on equity. We maintain Core allocations to 
China equities, with low valuations, and Korea equities, with 
explosive earnings growth in its semiconductors industry, but 
volatile price movements. We are Underweight ASEAN, 
which offers relatively lower earnings growth. 
What are the implications for equity sectors? 
We are Overweight these sectors globally: US 
information technology, US healthcare, US financials, US 
materials, Europe ex -UK financials, and Japan 
financials. 
A framework we use is to break the market into four key 
sector building blocks. Tech is the largest block, 
encompassing the Information Technology, Communication 
Services and Consumer Discretionary sectors. Put 
differently, the segment spans five major industries: 
semiconductors, hardware, internet, software, and IT 
services. Within the US market, we expect this segment to 
deliver c.30% earnings growth in 2027, extending the strong 
momentum seen in 2026. 
Financials and Real Estate  are the next building block, 
comprising these two sectors. We expect this segment to 
continue delivering low -teens earnings growth in 2027, 
supported by resilient economic activity and healthy credit 
trends. 
Resources include companies across four sectors: 
Industrials, Materials, Energy, and Utilities . We expect the 
segment to generate 10 -15% earnings growth in 2027, 
although Energy is likely to remain the largest drag on overall 
growth due to tougher comparisons and softer energy price 
assumptions for 2027. 
Healthcare & Others  consists primarily of Healthcare and 
Consumer Staples. We expect mid-teen earnings growth for 
the segment in 2027, with a rebound in healthcare earnings 
representing a potential upside surprise. 
Our Overweight sector exposures are drawn from each of the 
four key blocks above. Unlike the last few years , with 
concentrated gains from Tech providing market leadership, 
we see our downgrade in global equities also reflecting a 
broadening in market leadership towards each of the other 
sector blocks apart from Tech.

As structural themes such as AI transition from the rapid 
buildout phase towards monetisation, diversified exposure 
across sectors and regions should become increasingly 
important. 
What are the implications for equity 
opportunistic ideas? 
We have opportunistic ideas on Global High -Dividend 
equities, US healthcare, Euro area banks , MSCI 
Switzerland and Japan banks. The selection represents a 
more defensive stance  currently, with an average beta of 
below 1 relative to global equities (MSCI AC World), but 
attractive drivers nonetheless, for each of the ideas.  
Global High-Dividend offers higher-income equity allocation 
with historically lower volatility than global equities. US 
healthcare is supported by wider adoption of obesity 
treatments, advance s in oncology a nd innovative medical 
devices, that support earnings growth. Euro area banks  
continue to see steady earnings growth adding to their 
excess capital, which is being returned to shareholders via 
attractive dividend and share buyback yields. Japan banks 
continue to enjoy net margin expansion from BoJ policy 
normalisation, as well as corporate governance 
improvements. MSCI Switzerland is a newly initiated idea for 
investors seeking defensive exposure. H ealthcare and 
consumer staples account s for more than half the index 
weight, providing exposure to relatively resilient demand that 
is more insulated from economic cycles and AI trends. It also 
has a sizeable financial sector supported by steady wealth 
management growth and well-capitalised insurers with 
disciplined underwriting and resilient premium income. 
We see the case for more active management  in a mid -
cycle transition, as higher market dispersion should create 
greater opportunities for active sector and stock selection, 
particularly as leadership within equities evolves. 
What are the implications of higher equity 
market volatility?  
A more uncertain political backdrop and p otentially maturing 
growth rates in certain market segments may contribute to 
more frequent bouts of market volatility. Higher volatility 
presents an opportunity to use structured strategies to gain 
exposure to equities. Such strategies may be used to 
generate income or to gain exposure at a discounted entry 
price. Investors can also consider strategies that are 
structured to return principal or partial principal at maturity. 
What alternative strategies are suited to a 
regime of moderating equity market returns?  
Investors can consider alternative strategies such as hedge  
fund strategies or private markets, for those with the capacity

to accept some illiquidity.  
Equity long/short hedge fund  strategies would retain growth 
participation with less market exposure. Fund managers buy 
companies they expect to outperform and short those they 
expect to underperform, seeking to earn the difference 
between the two , and benefit even as total equity market 
returns are moderating. 
Within private markets , private equity provides  a means to 
higher returns with the effects of an illiquidity premium. 
Meanwhile, i nfrastructure offers  differentiated exposure to 
structural growth opportunities, where c ontracted long-term 
revenues reduce dependence on equity-market sentiment. 
What are the implications on fixed income 
and FX markets? 
Bonds: This year’s surge in bond yields means bonds now 
offer a hedge against any equity market weakness. We 
believe the 3 -7-year part of the US bond yield curve offers 
the best hedge against any equity sell -off caused by AI -
related growth worries. This part of the bond curve has priced 
in most Fed rate hike expectations, which could reverse if 
growth concerns return. Meanwhile, we favour US Treasury 
Inflation-Protected Securities (TIPS) as a hedge against any 
fuel price-led inflation scare.  
Credit: For corporate bonds, adopting a defensive stance in 
a volatile environment typically involves: i) increasing credit 
quality, favouring higher-rated issuers; and ii) moving up the 
capital structure, by reducing exposure to subordinated debt 
and favouring secured debt. From a sector perspective, 
large, diversified banks, utilities, and quasi -sovereigns tend 
to be more defensive, particularly relative to cyclical sectors, 
such as metals and mining, exploration, and technology.         
FX: We favour bearish AUD/JPY as a tactical hedge against 
any equity market correction. Review of 21 past S&P500 
correction sessions (2010 -2025) shows AUD weakened 
against USD in 86% of cases, while JPY strengthened 
against USD in 67%, supporting our bearish AUD/JPY 
outlook during any equity market stress.

## Document 350: GLOBAL MARKETS CALL_ Government Bond Yields Ease, Gold Falls

Source PDF: all report pdf/GLOBAL MARKETS CALL_ Government Bond Yields Ease, Gold Falls.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-01
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: GLOBAL MARKETS CALL: Government Bond Yields Ease, Gold Falls
Date:    43 minutes ago
GLOBAL MARKETS CALL: Government Bond Yields Ease, Gold Falls

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
GLOBAL MARKETS CALL:
Government Bond Yields Ease,
Gold Falls
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 1 Nov 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Gaël Gaborel - OrbisTerrae / Unsplash
Investors sought safety in government bonds last week as the AI selloff
rattled stock markets worldwide. Yields eased across most of the major
bond markets. The stock market selloff has been hardest on the markets
that soared the most this year. October is ending with the US well ahead
of the rest of the world. That validates our recent shift to market weight on
the Go Global portfolio strategy. Here’s more:

I. Stock Market Performance
The Stay Home vs Go Global ratios have rebounded sharply since mid-
year, driven by US outperformance (chart). They remain below their early
2025 peaks but are heading back up.
The US MSCI’s share of the All Country World MSCI’s market cap has
risen back to 65.3% (chart). Its share of forward earnings is 54.3%. That
11-point gap is the widest since January 2025. US stocks again are
pricing in far more than their share of global earnings.

### Page 3

October’s biggest losers were this year’s AI winners. South Korea finished
the month at the bottom of the country ETF rankings, down 16.4% in
dollars (chart). Taiwan fell 10.4%. Switzerland topped the board with an
8.8% gain, followed by Mexico (4.8%). The US slipped 0.9%, while the All
Country World ex-US ETF fell 2.9%.
II. Stock Markets Earnings & Valuation

### Page 4

Overseas forward earnings momentum is the strongest in over 15 years.
Forward earnings for the All Country World ex-US MSCI is up 41.7% y/y,
topping its 2021 post-pandemic peak (chart). Forward revenues is up
11.6% y/y. Earnings outpacing revenues by that much implies forward
profit margins are widening significantly.
Japan leads the major markets in forward earnings growth since early
2009 (chart). Its forward earnings has risen 720.7% in yen. The US is up

524.6%. EM forward earnings has surged this year and is now up 224.7%
over the same period. The EMU and the UK have barely doubled.
Valuation multiples are falling even as earnings soar (chart). The US
MSCI’s forward P/E has dropped to 19.1 from 23.7 a year ago. Japan’s is

### Page 5

14.7, and the EMU’s is 13.9. The UK trades at 11.9. EM trades at 8.9, its
lowest since the 2008 financial crisis.
III. Fixed Income
Government bond yields eased last week in most major markets in
response to the AI selloff (chart). The US 10-year Treasury yield has fallen
to 4.91% from 5.00% a week earlier. France’s 10-year government bond
yield has eased to 4.98%. The UK’s 5.30% and Australia’s 5.14% remain
the highest in the group. Japan’s yield has dropped to 2.76%.

IV. Gold
We've been bullish on the gold price since it jumped to a new record high
above $2,000 per ounce in early 2024. We reckoned that buying by the

### Page 6

central banks of countries that feared the US and Europe might freeze
their forex reserves, as happened to Russia after it invaded Ukraine,
which would be bullish for gold. That was true until the war started in the
Middle East on February 28, 2026 (chart).
We aren't sure why gold hasn't bounced off of its key support levels.
Instead, it dropped back below $4,000 last week. The next support is
around $3,400. Rising interest rates may explain why gold has been so

weak if central bank buying is being swamped by selling from private
investors.
The People's Bank of China is still buying gold (chart). Chinese investors
have lost lots of money in their property and stock markets. Now, they
may be bailing out of gold.

### Page 7

Is there still a case for gold to reach a price of $10,000 per ounce by the
end of the decade? Yes, but the case is getting weaker. If the S&P 500
reaches 10,000 by the end of the decade, investors might rebalance their
portfolios by allocating more to gold (chart).
V. Focus On China & Brazil
China’s MSCI stock price index has fallen to 66.4 in dollars, down from
92.0 at its 2025 high (chart). The property bust and deflation continue to
weigh on the market. So does the rapidly aging population.

Chinese tech has missed the AI boom entirely (chart). The China
Technology ETF (CQQQ) has fallen to 37.78, 65.2% below its February
2021 record high. The Nasdaq-100 ETF (QQQ) is 9.5% below its record
high. We continue to favor EM ex-China over China.

### Page 8

In Brazil, President Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva won the October 25 runoff
with nearly 52% of valid votes after trailing Senator Flávio Bolsonaro in the
first round. Investors had positioned for a Bolsonaro win, and Bolsonaro
says he will challenge the result in court. The Brazil MSCI stock price
index fell to 3,508.86 in local currency, giving back most of its post-first-
round gains (chart). It is now up just 1.0% ytd in local currency and 2.2%
in dollars.

## Document 354: How the blue wave could reshape US energy and data centres

Source PDF: all report pdf/How the blue wave could reshape US energy and data centres.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Energy aﬀordability shaped the midterms. A divided
government will now face pressure to shield consumers from
rising data centre power costs while speeding up new energy
supply. Onshoring and trade protections will continue, despite
higher near-term costs, as energy security remains a priority
Coco Zhang
After the midterm elections, the government will need to minimise data
centre-related costs for consumers, bring new power supply online faster and

protect supply chains. Pictured: Data centre in Ohio
Exit polls from the US midterm elections highlighted a key concern
shaping voters’ decisions: energy aﬀordability. Nearly 90% of
voters said the cost of living was a crucial or major factor in their
vote for Congress, with many pointing to gasoline and utility costs.
Meanwhile, as the AI data centre boom strains power supply and
infrastructure, 56% said the issue was a crucial or major factor in

their vote.
With Democrats now set to control both the House and Senate,
what does this mean for US energy and data centre policy?

### Page 2

Newly elected lawmakers will face pressure to address these
concerns, requiring the Democratic Congress and Republican White
House to ﬁnd common ground on shielding consumers from data
centre-related costs and accelerating new power supply, including
through permitting reform. Onshoring and supply chain protections
are also likely to continue. While they can raise near-term costs,
they remain important to US energy security. Developments in all
three areas could have signiﬁcant implications for businesses.

That said, Congress needs to strike a balance when drafting
legislation aimed at achieving these goals. More aggressive
measures could face a veto from President Trump.
Strengthened data centre regulation ahead,
but the devil is in the detail
The data centre industry is facing heightened local opposition.
Recent polls suggest that around one in seven Americans is
concerned about or opposes data centre development in their
neighbourhoods.
In response to this issue, there is likely to be tighter data centre

regulation. State-level policy will be more important to watch, as
action is politically and practically easier at this level. The most
likely approach is targeted regulation, much of which is already
emerging. This includes data centre-related taxes, reduced tax
incentives, environmental standards, and requirements for
developers to bear relevant cost increases. Outright moratoriums
remain unlikely to become mainstream. Where they are introduced,

they are more likely to be temporary, giving states time to develop
a data centre policy framework.
At the federal level, the key unanswered question is how much
authority should remain with the states and whether federal
legislation should mandate state regulations or simply provide
guidance. Republicans generally favour letting states lead, while
Democrats tend to support a stronger federal role.
We think sweeping data centre legislation is unlikely because of

President Trump’s veto power. Federal action is more likely to focus

on aﬀordability by protecting electricity customers from higher
costs and formalising data centres' responsibility to help fund grid
and energy infrastructure upgrades. Such measures could be added
to broader legislation, such as must-pass spending bills needed to
avoid a government shutdown, or a bipartisan package like
permitting reform.
More broadly on AI, we expect the Democratic Party to increase
scrutiny of technology companies, as discussed here, while utilising

the next two years to prepare an AI governance framework for 2028
and beyond.
However, tighter AI and data centre regulation is unlikely to
fundamentally alter the industry’s growth trajectory. AI’s
importance to US economic growth and competition with China will
remain powerful drivers of expansion.
Business implications
State-level policy would more directly aﬀect project siting,
which is important for data centre companies to watch.
Where state rules are tighter, project development could slow.

However, a more restrictive regulatory environment could
also increase the value of data centres already approved or
under construction, easing some of the industry’s capital
constraints. Higher valuations could also encourage
developers to be more selective and expand at a more
measured pace, ultimately improving the quality of the
project pipeline.
Developers should also treat community engagement as a
strategic investment. Recent surveys show that while only

20-30% of Americans support local data centre construction,
support can rise to 50-70% when there are accompanying
community investments, such as in public infrastructure
improvements or reductions in residents' costs. More
developers are now signing binding community beneﬁt
agreements as part of project development.

Enabling faster power generation through
permitting reform
Both parties support timely expansion of electricity supply to meet

rising demand from AI and manufacturing reshoring. They also
generally agree that energy abundance can help reduce cost
pressures. While Democrats and Republicans diﬀer on the preferred
energy mix, there is still common ground. Technologies likely to
retain bipartisan support include nuclear, geothermal, carbon
capture, and biofuels.
However, the greatest potential for policy action may lie not in
speciﬁc technologies, but in permitting reform.
Today, many energy projects can spend over ﬁve years navigating

the federal permitting process. Around $240bn to $280bn of
infrastructure investment across the eight sectors below enters the

Source: McKinsey & Company, ING Research
With both parties recognising the need for reform, the Senate
introduced the Bipartisan American Aﬀordability and Jobs Act
(BAAJA) in September 2026. Key permitting proposals include:

Speeding up approvals for energy projects
Providing greater certainty and consistency across
administrations
Making it easier to permit power transmission lines
Despite debate over its environmental impact, there is broad
agreement that the BAAJA would meaningfully improve the
permitting process. The key question is whether it can become law
under the current Congress. The Senate is expected to vote on 9
November, after which the bill would still need approval from the

House and President Trump. Democrats may want to delay the bill
and seek changes once they regain control of Congress next year,
but doing so could increase the risk of a presidential veto. This
creates an incentive to reach a deal sooner rather than later.

### Page 6

Even if the BAAJA does not pass under the current Congress,
permitting reform is likely to remain a bipartisan theme. The
growing need to expand power supply and infrastructure more
quickly will keep pressure on future Congresses to streamline
permitting.
Business implications
For energy companies – both renewables and fossil fuels –
the BAAJA could signiﬁcantly shorten permitting timelines. If
enacted, it would give project developers greater certainty,

reducing the risk of litigation and permit cancellations. This
could further expand project siting options for developers.
Nevertheless, in the very near term, projects may need to
retain ﬂexibility around their permitting strategy while the
new policy framework is developed and implemented.
Expect more protectionism and reshoring
efforts against China dominance
From clean energy technologies to the critical minerals that
underpin much of the US economy, China controls a large share of

global production capacity. Consequently, reducing reliance on
China will remain a US policy priority. Such eﬀorts would increase
rather than reduce project costs in the near term. But the long-term
beneﬁts of stronger domestic manufacturing and more secure
supply chains could outweigh those costs.
Global supply chain capacity distribution
across various sectors

Source: ING Research, BloombergNEF, International Energy Agency
This creates two key risks. First, heavy reliance on China leaves US
industries vulnerable to geopolitical tensions. We have already seen
this with rare earths, where Chinese export restrictions contributed
to disruptions for US auto manufacturers. Second, China’s supply
chain dominance can put US manufacturers at a competitive
disadvantage.
Building more secure energy and natural resource supply chains

has long attracted bipartisan support in the US. The Trump
administration has supported domestic rare earth production
through funding, purchase agreements, and equity investments. In
solar, China-focused trade and tax credit restrictions are also
supporting the expansion of domestic manufacturing.
We expect this focus to continue. The executive branch could
pursue further protective measures and is unlikely to face major
opposition from Congress. Congress could also authorise additional

funding to strengthen rare earth supply chains.
Furthermore, a Democratic-controlled Congress could push to
extend clean manufacturing tax credits as part of broader
negotiations with President Trump. Given the administration’s focus
on countering China and expanding US manufacturing, an
extension could emerge as a compromise in a broader legislative
package under an optimistic scenario. All these eﬀorts would also
extend into future administrations.

Business implications
For US companies:
Clean energy companies and other industries that rely on
rare earths, including semiconductors, AI, defence, and

electronics, should have enough policy certainty to build
long-term supplier relationships outside China. In the near
term, however, diversiﬁcation could raise supply chain costs
as alternative sources may be more expensive. Solar could
face particular cost pressures, as some tariﬀs on supply
chain equipment also apply to non-Chinese suppliers.
For overseas companies:
US eﬀorts to build supply chains outside China are creating
opportunities for manufacturers elsewhere. The US is

supporting rare earth production in Australia and Brazil, while
solar and battery suppliers in countries such as South Korea
and Japan could beneﬁt from stronger demand. This could
also encourage more manufacturing investment in the US.
Meanwhile, European clean energy developers could face
greater competition for non-Chinese supplies. While the EU
has not imposed US-style tariﬀs, it is tightening restrictions
on Chinese equipment through funding, procurement,

auction, and subsidy rules. This could intensify global
competition for non-Chinese suppliers and push costs higher.

## Document 357: India_ Degrees of rupee defence (2026-10-16)

Source PDF: all report pdf/India_ Degrees of rupee defence (2026-10-16).pdf
Report source: DBS
Date: 2026-10-16

### Page 1

TAKING STOCK

The Reserve Bank of India (RBI) has adopted a 
multi-layered defence strategy to support the 
rupee this year.

This has entailed a combination of outright 
intervention in spot/ forwards, regulatory 
action, liquidity steps, administrative measures 
and moves to curb import -related dollar 
demand.

The rupee has appreciated by 1.8% against the 
dollar in the past five days since recent 
measures took effect, paring YTD depreciation 
to 5.5% . High oil prices, weakness in capital 
inflows, a firm dollar , tariffs/ geopolitical 
worries, energy , and risk -off sentiments have 
kept the INR under pressure since last year.

India: Degrees of rupee defence

16 October 2026 
Radhika Rao, Senior Economist 
radhikarao@dbs.com

### Page 2

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            2

ECHOES OF 2013 WITH FEW 
DIFFERENCES

Comparisons with 2013 are apt, though 
the backdrop differs.  As for the 
catalysts, markets reacted to the US 
Fed’s taper announcement in 2013, 
which pushed US yields higher  and led 
global investors to reduce exposure to 
vulnerable EM economies. As a result, 
capital inflows weakened, exposing 
funding mismatch es for countries, 
including India, that were running wide 
current account deficits.

In 2026, markets face the spectre of 
elevated oil prices, geopolitical 
uncertainty, global competition for 
capital, a firm dollar ( DXY appreciated 
+3.8% YTD) , and US inflation/ deficits 
which have settled at levels much higher 
than pre -pandemic years. This has 
pushed up UST yields, and the US Fed 
kicked off its rate-hike cycle in Sep26.

Against this backdrop, the RBI is 
pursuing the same objective , i.e., to 
ensure rupee stability, but the nature of 
the battle differs. The defence strategy 
has shifted from ‘crisis management’ 
mode in 2013 , due to weaker domestic 
macroeconomic balances (a wide current 
account gap, high inflation, a lower 
reserve buffer, and wide fiscal deficits) , 
to a ‘terms-of-trade shock and impact of 
a firm dollar’ in 2026.

Flashpoints are  nonetheless similar, 
with reliance on portfolio financing , 
leaving the external account vulnerable 
to shifts in global risk sentiment. 
Portfolio outflows have emerged as the 
primary transmission channel through 
which external shocks have translated 
into INR depreciation pressure , 
compounded by high energy prices and a 
firm dollar.

FX PLAYBOOK

While the response framework is similar 
between 2013 and this year, the broad 
sequence and nature of macroprudential 
measures differ. This year’s multi-
layered rupee defence toolkit has 
included:

-Direct FX defence  by way of 
intervention in the spot and forwards 
market. As of Aug26, the net forward 
dollar liabilities ballooned to $200bn, 
rising by ~$63bn vs . the month before . 
Recent sell/buy FX swaps are expected 
to lower this stock in the coming months.

-Attracting dollar inflows  by way of 
special swap facilities for FCNR deposits, 
and offshore borrowings , apart from 
liberalising taxes around debt inflows,

### Page 3

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            3

amongst others. We discussed this here, 
here, here and here.

Taking a proactive stance, a portion of 
existing FX reserves could be earmarked 
against forward liabilities (from maturing 
FCNR deposits) , helping to mitigate 
concerns that deposit maturities or debt 
repayments could trigger a sharp
increase in dollar ⁠demand down the line.

-Domestic liquidity management. Bonds, 
to some degree, are paying the price for 
the authorities' efforts to defend the 
rupee against the additional headwind of 
weak global conditions.  INR liquidity 
surged on the back of the swap windows, 
prompting the RBI to undertake open 
market operations, VRRR auctions, sell -
buy swaps, among others. While these 
have been effective in absorbing 
balances, bond yields have drifted higher. 
10Y yields are at three-year highs and are 
expected to climb further  (DBSf: 7.40 -
7.50% by year-end).

-Moderate FX demand and speculation . 
Earlier in the year, the RBI had 
introduced change s to the banks’ net 
open position framework, and thereafter 
partially rolled back a few curbs. 
Prudential measures on FX exposures 
and hedging -related measures were 
announced in Oct26, which include d the 
introduction of a daily cash reserve with 
the RBI equal to 20% of the rupee 
equivalent of the notional value for INR -
linked derivative contracts exceeding 
$2mn used to hedge current account 
transactions, referred to as the Foreign 
Exchange Risk Reserve (FERR) . 
Subsequent clarifications provided 
operational details on where the FERR 
would be maintained and that it would be 
subject to audits.

Since 12 Oct, the RBI has been providing 
dollars via designated banks to the three 
state-owned oil -marketing companies 
(Indian Oil, BPCL, and HPCL) to meet the 
their daily dollar requirements, thereby 
diverting related spot-market demand.

On hedging-related matters, the limit for 
undertaking foreign exchange derivative 
transactions without proving an 
underlying commercial exposure was 
cut to $5mn from $100mn across all ADs 
and exchange -traded currency 
derivatives. Restrictions on Rebooking , 
i.e., a uthorized dealers are prohibited 
from allowing customers to rebook 
cancelled rupee -linked derivative 
contracts (though rollovers upon 
maturity remain permitted). Lastly, 
banks must obtain written confirmation 
from users that the same underlying 
exposure has not been hedged with 
another dealer.  Data from CCI , cited by

### Page 4

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            4

Bloomberg, pointed to importers’ 
forward dollar purchases rising 44% on 
the year to a monthly average of $64bn.

-Current account management . Efforts 
to contain structural FX outflows by way 
of higher duties and taxes on gold 
imports discourage non -essential 
purchases and production incentives to 
improve export competitiveness.

IMPACT

To assess whether the playbook has 
worked, we observe:

a) Rupee has enjoyed a brief respite. 
Despite oil prices at $105/bbl at the time 
of writing and elevated global yields, 
USDINR fell from near 96.80 to wards 
95.00, owing to measures announced in 
Oct26 following the central bank’s 25bp 
hike. Beyond temporary relief, global 
catalysts are likely to be back to the fore 
and weigh on the currency (see chart);

b) Sharp inflows under the swap 
facilities pushed the foreign reserves 
stock to a record high, providing 
policymakers sufficient buffer to 
navigate future shocks from a position of 
strength. A strong interventionist 
response has since led to a softening in 
the overall stock (see chart).

c) Macroprudential measures have led to 
distortions in other parts of the market . 
For instance, strong swap -related 
inflows had inorganically pushed 
onshore INR liquidity up sharply, 
necessitating absorption measures. 
Similarly, NOP restrictions and the more 
recent tighter requirements on hedging 
activity and counterparty transactions  
had some after -effects. Forward points 
have surged this week , with financial 
institutions pointing to relative illiquidity 
in the currency forwards market  on 
volumes and activity basis, and fewer 
avenues to deal with dollar supply.

### Page 5

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            5

d) Swap-related windows are a timely 
support for the country’s balance of 
payments position. From expectations of 
a third consecutive year of BOP deficit in 
FY27, the math has now swung to a 
potential surplus, even as current 
account worries remain. With energy 
prices settling north of $100/bbl, 
keeping the monthly trade deficit near 
$30bn, our FY27 current account deficit 
forecast of 1.1% of GDP faces  upside 
risks of around 0.3-0.5% of GDP. We note 
the s harp jump in ECB outflows in S ep, 
though see that as a temporary overshot 
of leverage-FCNR activity.

WHAT LIES AHEAD

Authorities are likely not done yet and 
remain ready to take further action, as 
required. RBI Governor Malhotra 
reiterated the authorities’ serious intent 
of stabilising the currency in a recent 
interview (Moneycontrol, 13 Oct), 
emphasising that “The RBI is determined 
to do whatever is necessary to ensure 
orderly movement in the foreign 
exchange market,”. He also highlighted 
that recent steps were not tantamount to 
capital control measures that were 
aimed at ensuring that forex markets 
serve genuine hedging needs rather than 
speculative bets.  Market participants 
opine that FX volumes and interest are 
likely to be subdued in a few pockets on 
the back of the recent measures, 
consequently stabilizing the rupee.

Unlike in 2013, when policy tightening 
was a part of the initial response, the 
2026 strategy has prioritised targeted 
FX and regulatory measures.  The 
sequencing suggests a deliberate effort 
to distinguish actions aimed at 
preserving currency stability from those 
intended to achieve domestic monetary 
objectives, with rates remaining a 
potential, though not immediate, tool in 
the defence framework.

However, global developments have 
altered that dynamic . Rising US yields 
and a gradual increase in the US Fed 
Funds rate (DBS baseline: a total of 75bp 
hikes) have compressed IN -US spreads. 
The recent sell -off in INR benchmark

### Page 6

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            6

bonds has effectively widened Gsec -
UST spreads across maturities, in 
contrast to the compression witnessed in 
regional high-yielders.

The latest announcements may not 
fully quell speculation over further 
policy action.

Upcoming measures might include -

-Markets anticipate relief on the long -
term capital gains tax structure on 
equities to draw in longer-duration 
inflows. This measure will boost inflows 
if it coincides with other factors that 
make domestic equities attractive for 
thematic investing , as global risk -free 
rates moderate.

-Street expectations also include steps 
to boost inward dollar repatriation by 
exporters to boost domestic dollar 
supply.

-Wider policy rate corridor akin to 2013.

-Policy rate response. While this might 
not be a panacea, it is a likely necessary 
condition. Raising the neutral rate might 
have mixed success in attracting capital 
market inflows ; though rate-sensitive 
flows are likely to get a fillip as investors 
seek attractive rate differentials in the 
region.

An adequate real rate cushion would 
also align with the evolving domestic 
inflation realities, which are expected to 
accelerate to 6% in the Oct-Dec quarter 
and remain above 5% well into 2027.

Given fluid global developments, we 
outline three scenarios on the rate path 
ahead. Our baseline assumption  is for 
75bp hikes in this cycle  (including Oct), 
spread between the Dece mber and 
February 2027 meetings. We don’t rule 
out an intermeeting rate hike before the 
December meeting, contingent on 
rupee movements.

Under the second scenario , s hould 
depreciation pressures on the rupee 
return, the 50bp hike might be brought 
forward to December , with another in 
February, taking cumulative hikes in this 
cycle to 100bp. The t hird scenario  is a 
more aggressive 150bp worth of hikes in 
this cycle, with 50bp front -loaded in 
December, and the rest spilling into 2027. 
Factors that  will determine whether 
these scenarios materialise are the 
resumption and scale of rupee 
depreciation, oil price direction, US Fed 
Funds rate increases (DBSf: total 75bps) 
and other risk-off events.

Path ahead

Despite these measures, we see limited 
room for sustained INR appreciation on 
grounds of manufacturing/ trade 
competitiveness. A challenging flows 
portfolio flows outlook leaves the d oor 
open to further gradual rupee 
depreciation subject to global 
developments, with high sensitivity to oil 
price movements and risk sentiments . A 
strong rate response will help introduce  
favourable rate differentials and reduce 
currency volatility.

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026

ECONOMICS & STRATEGY

► 美國財政部 20年期公債拍賣結果顯示需求低
於預期 ，財政部標售 20年期公債共 130億美
元，最高得標殖利率達 5.469%，為1986年1
月以來20年期公債拍賣最高利率 ，長天期美
債需求仍疲 。信用債市面對 AI大額發債與資
金回收效率擔憂 ，信用債利差一度擴大 ，隨
企業財報揭幕 ，市場資金流入具防禦性質的
高信評債種 ，如美國投資級債與 亞洲投資級
債表現相對抗跌。
► 美伊衝突可能進一步擴大 ，美國公布截至上
周原油庫存減少 310萬桶 ，降幅高於市場預
期，地緣風險難消與原油供需失衡 壓力，油
價震盪上行 。加密貨幣市場亦見反彈 ，美國
貨幣監理署 (OCC)發布公告敲 定GENIUS法
案最終實施細則 ，疊加近期機構投資者對加
密貨幣接受度 提升推動近期 漲勢。匯市主要
受到美伊戰事添通膨變數 ，投資人靜 待下週
聯準會利率決策結果，美元相對承壓。

示聯準會將升息一碼 且時點可能在 12月，未
來1年則存在3碼潛在升息空間 ，考量地緣衝
突短期 內持 續 ， 若 通膨難 以改善 至政 策目
標，預期聯準會2027年將維持緊縮貨幣政策

心物價發展，引導短天期公債利率走向。

境影響 甚微，反映美 股和信用 市場低波動狀
態，體現實質 政策利率低於 緊縮利率 區間與
彭博金 融情勢指數變化不大 ，當前美國貨幣
政策與市場流動性仍偏寬鬆 ，倘若地緣擾動

持續造成通 膨居高難下 ，恐進一步推升聯準
會升息預期 ，疊加政府財政 風險與貨幣政策
透明度降 ，加劇長 天期公債利率 上行風險 ，
長天期債以交易為主，不建議長期持有。

► 特斯拉3Q26財報好壞參半 — 毛利率與交付
量超預 期 ， 營收 大 致符合 ，營業 利潤 遜預
期，最大意外是自由現金流達 15億美元，高
於共識負43億，資本開支指引下調成憂慮。
► 早前公司展示下代 Roadster能力，本質上是
光環車 ，定價高受眾少 ，2028 年才有望投
產，盈利貢獻有限 。Model 3/Y成為大眾商
品，競爭激烈 ，利潤率壓縮 。正如傳統品牌
以賽道或參賽展示技術 ，「飛天」旨在展示
獨有跨界整合能力，維持品牌形象溢價。
► 至於和SpaceX的潛在合併，核心在於貫通多
頂 業 務， 提 高 資 本 運 用 效 率， 共同分擔
Terafab 的開支等 ，現時兩家合計市值約為
3.5兆美元。這項巨型潛在交易 ，除了要跨過
極高表決門檻外，SpaceX具極敏感的美國國
防及NASA合作背景 ，而特斯拉有超過 20%
業務倚重上海超級工廠 ，合併存在嚴重地緣
衝突。預期馬斯克將在川普離任前 ，加速執
行Tesla與SpaceX 合併。

► 今年9月適逢地緣擾動持續推升高油價、聯準
會重啟 升息 、財政部 與企 業發債 浪潮 等 影
響，形塑債市今年以來單月最差表現 。檢視
2021年以來美國投資級債前十大單月最差總
報酬，2022年聯準會 3月啟動升息年度就佔
一半以上，顯示利率風險仍為近5年來債市最
大風險 ，然檢視單月最差報酬表現未來期間
總報酬，除2022年1月和3月聯準會重啟升息
前夕與當月表現明顯較弱，多數於未來1年後
多呈現正報酬 ，反映隨利率走升放大孳息效
益，同時挹注債券長期持有之報酬空間。
► 今年來債券市場利率波動明顯推升信用債利
差，其 中僅 以低 信 評債利 差升幅 較顯 ，此
外，9月以來利率連動度高及AI相關產業債種
也成為利差擴大重災區 ，凸顯債市投資人減
輕市場不確定性的投資傾向 ，考量信用債市
歷經利率上行利空洗禮 ，信用利差同步擴大

創造收益空間 ，可把握債信體質仍佳企業債
鎖利良機，並透過時間複利力量強化收益。
利率波動升，僅低信評利差擴大程度相對明顯9月為美國投資級債今年最差單月報酬
用時間換空間，單月最差表現形塑未來報酬機會

### Page 8

8
► 受聯準會重啟升息影響 ，過去一年美國公債
殖利率曲線持續陡峭化 ，其中利率最大升幅
落在短天期的 2至3年期間，美國核心通膨與
就業仍具韌性 ，聯準會將持續保持 緊縮貨幣
政策步調來應對高通膨 ，考量美國高物價與
高利率環境短期 難消，宜透過縮短債券配置
天期並進行動態調整 ，為因應後續利率不確
定性環境的良策。
► 為因應利率不確定性 ，投資人可採取債券梯
的投資配置 ，主要選取不同到期期間及不同
類型債劵進行 組合，債券梯投資重點主要在
於，根據殖利率曲線型態及利率動態建立不
同期間 債券梯，假設市場利率走升且殖利率
曲線正斜率 ，建立短天期債券梯除了降低利
率風險 ，有 短期 閒 置資金 及孳息 需求 投資
人，亦可取得穩定孳息 收益。若後續投資人
有短期資金需求 ，可以視情況將短期債券到
期後資金回收 ，降低只投資單一債券須持至
到期或直接變現的流動性風險。
投資焦點
美國公債殖利率曲線持續為正斜率
高利率波動持續，用策略迎戰市場擾動
資料來源：Bloomberg，凱基證券整理

建立短天期債券梯，分散風險及穩定現金流
債券梯
經過第2年第一檔 債券到期，視資金需求 及市況與利率 動
態決定後續配置

短天期債券梯 ：假設買入 2檔各 3、4、5及7年到期債券 ，
聚焦 短期穩定收益
► 債券梯是一種分散投資方式 ，主要選取不同到期期間及不同
類型債劵進行組合，可兼具：
1) 分散利率風險： 將資金分別買進不同到期年期的債券 ，以分
散利率風險。
2) 分散信用風險： 分散投資不同發行 機構債券，不會重壓 單一
債券並可分散信用違約風險。
3) 降低流動性風險： 若投資人有短期資金需求 ，可以視情況將
短期債券到期後資金回收 ，降低只投資單一債券須持至到期
或直接變現之流動性風險。

### Page 9

9
股市
 美股第三季獲利年增率預估為雙位數成長 ，市場靜待財報結果與展望 ，和對AI資本支出回收影響 。
考量地緣衝突 、殖利率偏高、評價面壓力與鄰近美國期中選舉 ，短期金融市場紛擾不止 ，可增加防
禦股增強耐震度 。長期AI、科技發展趨勢不變 ，逢股市拉回時可分批布局 ，分散配置於不同國家與
產業，降低市場整體波動，亦可以槓鈴策略(防禦+成長)穩中求利。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，全球利率走揚有利銀行股 。此外，地緣衝突頻傳 ，歐洲國防軍工仍
有投資題材。
策略風格：可增加防禦股配置，中長期看
好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分關注
醫療保健生技產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升 ，預估今年底前主要國家仍有升息空間 ，殖利率偏高環境 使中短天期債券提供
吸引力，逢利率走高、鎖利領息。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流與還款能力 ，聚焦風險調整後
利差具吸引力者，包含金融、通訊及公用事業等產業。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、通訊及公用事業等

匯市
 歐洲政治風險上升、歐元短期弱盤，美元高檔有撐。
 日本銀行再度升息時間不定，日圓區間震盪。
美元高檔有撐
商品
 中東地緣衝突短期難解，油價維持高位但震盪加劇。
 短期美國雙率走高 ，使金價短期承壓 ，惟美歐日財政赤字壓力與去美元化 ，使全球央行與長期投資
人買盤依舊，提供黃金中長期穩定支撐。
短期黃金有壓，中長期維持偏多看法

### Page 18

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

This Week in Earnings provides analysis and commentary on aggregate  
earnings estimate revisions, growth rates and valuations.

ANALYST: Tajinder Dhillon, CFA October 30, 2026 
tajinder.dhillon@lseg.com; 
THIS WEEK IN EARNINGS 
AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS 
 26Q3 Y/Y earnings are expected to be 31.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the Y/Y earnings estimate is 26.4%. 
 Of the 299 companies in the S&P 500 that have reported earnings to date for 26Q3, 81.6% have reported earnings 
above analyst estimates. This compares to a long-term average of 67.7% and prior four quarter average of 81.5%. 
 26Q3 Y/Y revenue is expected to be 13.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the growth estimate is 12.2%. 
 69.9% of companies have reported 26Q3 revenue above analyst expectations. This compares to a long-term 
average of 63% and an average over the past four quarters of 76.8%. 
 For 26Q3, there have been 40 negative EPS preannouncements issued by S&P 500 corporations compared to 66 
positive EPS preannouncements. By dividing 40 by 66 the N/P ratio is 0.6 for the S&P 500 Index.  
 The forward four-quarter (26Q4– 27Q3) P/E ratio for the S&P 500 is 19.0. 
 During the week of Nov. 2, 144 S&P 500 companies are expected to report quarterly earnings.

THIS WEEK IN EARNINGS 
This Week in Earnings provides analysis and commentary on aggregate earnings estimate revisions, growth rates and valuations.  2 
There has been an increase in the share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 since the start of the quarter (to $778.4B 
from $760.7B). Three of the eleven sectors have experienced downward revisions to estimates.

Since Aug. 1, the communication services (-20.5%) and materials (-7%) sectors have recorded the highest percentage 
decreases in earnings, while the energy (33%) sector has recorded the highest percentage increase in earnings. 
Overall, share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 2.3% since the start of the quarter.

Since Aug. 1, the communication services (-14.7B) and materials (-1.2B) sectors have recorded the highest dollar-level 
decreases in earnings, while the energy (15.7B) sector has recorded the highest dollar-level increase in earnings. 
Overall, expected share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 17.7B since the start of the quarter.

companies beat the estimates and 15% missed estimates. 
In aggregate, companies are reporting earnings that are 4.5% above estimates, which compares to a long-term (since 
1994) average surprise factor of 4.4% and the average surprise factor over the prior four quarters of 7.7%.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the S&P 500 for 26Q3 is 31.6%. If the energy sector is excluded, the growth 
rate declines to 26.4%. The S&P 500 expects to see share-weighted earnings of $778.4B in 26Q3, compared to share-
weighted earnings of $591.4B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 503 constituents) in 25Q3. 
11 of the 11 sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to 25Q3. The Energy and 
Information Technology sectors have the highest earnings growth rates for the quarter, while the Consumer 
Discretionary sector has the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q3. 
The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (146.2%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $63B in 26Q3, 
compared to earnings of $25.6B in 25Q3. 4 of the 5 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher earnings 
than a year ago. The Oil & Gas Refining & Marketing (566.5%) and Integrated Oil & Gas (143.8%) sub-industries have 
the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate declines to 39%. 
The Information Technology sector has the second highest earnings growth rate (68.9%) of any sector. It is expected 
to earn $260.2B in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $154B in 25Q3. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are 
anticipated to see higher earnings than a year ago. The Semiconductors (137%) and Semiconductor Materials & 
Equipment (71.5%) sub-industries have the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, 
the growth rate declines to 28.2%. 
The Consumer Discretionary sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (3.9%) of any sector. It is expected to earn 
$56.3B in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $54.2B in 25Q3. 7 of the 18 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to 
see lower earnings than a year ago. The Casinos & Gaming (-44.1%) and Automotive Parts & Equipment (-38.8%) 
sub-industries have the lowest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate 
improves to 4.5%.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the S&P 500 for 26Q4 is 29.7%. If the energy sector is excluded, the growth 
rate declines to 25%. The S&P 500 expects to see share-weighted earnings of $810.7B in 26Q4, compared to share-
weighted earnings of $625.1B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 503 constituents) in 25Q4. 
11 of the 11 sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to 25Q4. The Energy and 
Information Technology sectors have the highest earnings growth rates for the quarter, while the Industrials sector has 
the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q4. 
The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (145.6%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $59.5B in 26Q4, 
compared to earnings of $24.2B in 25Q4. 3 of the 5 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher earnings 
than a year ago. The Oil & Gas Refining & Marketing (366.3%) and Integrated Oil & Gas (175.7%) sub-industries have 
the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate declines to 52.1%. 
The Information Technology sector has the second highest earnings growth rate (56.3%) of any sector. It is expected 
to earn $292.1B in 26Q4, compared to earnings of $186.8B in 25Q4. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are 
anticipated to see higher earnings than a year ago. The Semiconductors (119.5%) and Semiconductor Materials & 
Equipment (72.3%) sub-industries have the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, 
the growth rate declines to 19.1%. 
The Industrials sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (3.9%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $52B in 26Q4, 
compared to earnings of $50.1B in 25Q4. 2 of the 20 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see lower earnings 
than a year ago. The Aerospace & Defense (-39.6%) and Passenger Airlines (-29.8%) sub-industries have the lowest 
earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate improves to 30.5%.

The estimated revenue growth rate for the S&P 500 for 26Q3 is 13.6%. If the energy sector is excluded, the growth rate declines  to 12.2%. The S&P 
500 expects to see revenue of $4898.8B in 26Q3, compared to revenue of $4310.4B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 503 
constituents) in 25Q3.

11 of the 11 sectors anticipate revenue growth for the quarter. The Information Technology sector has the highest revenue growth rate for the 
quarter, while the Financials sector has the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q3.

The Information Technology sector has the highest revenue growth rate (40.7%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $772.3B in 26Q3, compared to 
revenue of $548.8B in 25Q3. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher revenue than a year ago. The Semiconductors 
(88%) and Semiconductor Materials & Equipment (49.4%) sub-industries have the highest revenue growth in the sector. If these su b-industries are 
removed, the growth rate declines to 23.7%.

The Financials sector has the lowest revenue growth rate (5.1%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $576.7B in 26Q3, compared  to revenue of 
$548.4B in 25Q3. 3 of the 13 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see lower revenue than a year ago. The Diversified  Financial Services (-
40.9%) and Reinsurance (-18.1%) sub-industries have the lowest revenue growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are remove d, the growth rate 
improves to 6%.

STARMINE EARNINGS SURPRISE FORECAST
Looking forward at quarterly performance, we use StarMine’s SmartEstimate® from LSEG to determine which
companies in the S&P 500 are better poised to beat and miss earnings estimates. The SmartEstimate® is a weighted

average of analyst estimates, with more weight given to more recent estimates and more accurate analysts. Our
studies have shown that when the SmartEstimate® differs significantly from the consensus (I/B/E/S mean estimate),
the Predicted Surprise accurately predicts the direction of earnings surprises or further revisions 70% of the time.
When a significant Predicted Surprise for revenue is also present for the period, the accuracy improves to 78%.

StarMine ARM from LSEG is an analyst revisions stock ranking model, designed to predict future changes in analyst
sentiment. ARM incorporates more accurate earnings estimates through the SmartEstimate prediction service. ARM
region rankings scores companies by region on a scale of 1 to 100 where 100 represents the most bullish sentiment.
Over the next two weeks, 147 S&P 500 companies are expected to report earnings. Of these companies, 17 positive

surprises and 11 negative surprises are expected from S&P 500 companies reporting quarterly results.

## Document 410: Taiwan Earnings Call Weekly Recap - Is Earnings Still Enough to Drive Stock Price_ _ GSTW

Source PDF: all report pdf/Taiwan Earnings Call Weekly Recap - Is Earnings Still Enough to Drive Stock Price_ _ GSTW.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Earnings Call Weekly Recap - Is Earnings
Still Enough to Drive Stock Price? | GSTW
30 October 2026 | 4:17 PM Taiwan Standard Time
Robert Wang
Goldman Sachs (Asia) L.L.C. Taipei Branch
FICC & Equities
Short week but busy for Taiwan in terms of earnings. Index is only down 1.8% WTD,
though multiple large caps saw over 10% drawdown in this week alone. The elevated
level in Taiwan vs other regional markets still attracted much short interest without

much buying interest except from retail. Overall sentiment is not bearish, though
investors have turned much more defensive and hiding in sectors such as Financials,
Telecom, and TSMC.
For earnings, winners' momentum was short lived; instead of chasing into the beat,
many chose to further profit take on the strength (Unimicron, EMC/TUC, Landmark
on open right after their earnings). Companies that missed the results were definitely
punished (i.e. Chroma, Delta, UMC). Echoing back to my previous note, range bound

trade seems to be the safest way to stay afloat.
See below for my single stock takeaway:
Mediatek (2454 TT)
Financial Highlights:
3Q26 revenue reached NT$168.06bn (+10% QoQ and +18% YoY) with EPS at
NT$17.5 (+15% QoQ and +11% YoY) vs cons. NT$15.95. GM came in at 46.3%
(+11ppt QoQ and 18ppt YoY) vs cons. 46.2%.
3Q26 outperformance was mainly from stronger-than-expected customer
demand and improved supply chain yield. First ASIC already started shipping since

Sep'26.
Operational Outlook:
Revenue outlook for 4Q26 is NT$210 - NT$222bn, translating to a 29% sequential
growth. 2027 revenue growth will likely grow by high single digits yoy. GM will be
around 45% with +/-1.5%, and OPM is expected to be at 26% +/-2%.
ASIC chip sales for 2026 will be around US$2.5bn, higher than previous guidance,
and it will reach US$16bn in 2027, around 20% of ASIC TAM and at the higher end
of the company guidance. Next gen chip's tape out is on track and targeted to

enter mass production in 1Q28.
Delta Electronics (2308 TT)
10/30/26, 4:46 PM Taiwan Earnings Call Weekly Recap - Is Earnings Still Enough to Drive Stock Price? | GSTW
https://marquee.gs.com/s/content/markets/2971c821-c2cc-403a-acfe-2b906dd125c4 1/11

### Page 2

Financial Highlights:
3Q26 revenue reached NT$197.1bn (+8% QoQ / +31% YoY), while EPS registered
at NT$9.05. GM declined to 34.1% (down 1.5ppt QoQ), impacted by a NT$3.9bn
inventory write-down (~1.7% of revenue) mostly in Power Electronics.
Operating profit rose +7% QoQ (+31% YoY) backed by operating leverage, keeping
OPM resilient at 16.6% flat QoQ. Net profit was NT$23.5bn (-7% QoQ / +26% YoY)
due to lower non-operating investment gains.
Operational Outlook:

Management expects 4Q26 revenue to grow sequentially with gross margins
unlikely to deteriorate, aided by inventory normalization and potential price
adjustments to offset ~2% YTD material cost inflation.
AI-related exposure approaches 50% of total revenue (liquid cooling at ~13%), and
management remains confident in AI infrastructure demand through 2030 despite
near-term bottlenecks.
Key Q&A:
Inventory Write-down & Normalization: The NT$3.9bn write-down stemmed from

component mismatches and slower customer ramp-ups of newer platforms (e.g.,
18.5kW vs. 5.5kW platforms); management expects affected inventory to be
progressively consumed by 1Q27.
HVDC Adoption & Vicor Litigation: While 400V products saw partial shipments in
3Q26, 800V adoption is slower and primarily NVIDIA-driven. On the Vicor VPD
patent dispute, management expects no near-term impact on customer
relationships or shipments, with an initial ITC determination unlikely until 2027.

ASE Technology Holding (3711 TT)
Financial Highlights:
3Q26 consolidated revenue reached NT$244.16bn, with a GM of 22.1% and EPS of
NT$5.84, all outperforming market expectations.
ATM (Advanced Semiconductor Engineering, Test and Materials) GM hit 30.6%,
breaking past previous structural highs; the company announced 30% as its
baseline gross margin for the future.
Operational Outlook:
4Q26 consolidated revenue is projected to grow 6%–8% QoQ, with GM between

23.0%–24.0% and OPM between 14.5%–15.5%.
CPO is set to enter mass production in 4Q26, with related revenue expected to
contribute over US$500mn in 2027; the full-year LEAP revenue target has been
revised upward to over US$4bn.
Key Q&A:
Capex Upward Revision: Due to strong demand for LEAP and mainstream
advanced packaging, full-year 2026 capex was raised from US$10.5bn to
US$11.5bn (60% allocated to LEAP, 40% to mainstream packaging), with 2027
capex expected to be even higher.

Capacity Bottlenecks & Expansion: Factory floor space is currently the primary
constraint on near-term growth. The company is actively searching for and
acquiring existing facilities to shorten setup timelines, prioritizing resource
allocation toward packaging and wafer sort.
Elite Material (2383 TT)

https://marquee.gs.com/s/content/markets/2971c821-c2cc-403a-acfe-2b906dd125c4 2/11

### Page 3

Financial Highlights:
Driven by strong demand, actual 3Q26 revenue grew 29% QoQ, comfortably
beating prior conservative expectations.
GM outperformed expectations, hitting 37.7% for the quarter.
Operational Outlook:
4Q26 revenue growth is conservatively estimated to be at least 15%–20% QoQ,
with gross margins expanding further to 38.0%–39.5%.
Proactive capacity expansion plans (targeting 15.25 million sheets/month by end-
2030 vs. 5.85 million sheets/month currently) are still insufficient to support all

signed Long-Term Agreements (LTAs) for the next 4 years, pointing to a severe
supply-demand gap and zero oversupply risk.
Key Q&A:
CCL Pricing & Substrate Expansion: EMC is executing its second round of high-end
CCL (M7+) price hikes this year (5%–20% increases covering M7/M8 and M8+/M9
grades). Furthermore, it is rapidly expanding high-end ABF substrate core-layer
CCL capacity from 300k sheets/month to at least 1.2 million sheets/month by end-
2027, targeting commercial shipments starting in 4Q27.

Competition & Market Share: While AI customers are qualifying second or third
sources to secure supply chains, EMC maintains its long-term target of holding a
50% AI CCL market share, supported by best-in-class product quality and deep
CSP/PCB collaboration.
Yageo Corporation (2327 TT)
Financial Highlights:
3Q26 revenue reached NT$49.53bn (+11.4% QoQ, +49.7% YoY), with a GM of 41%
and EPS of NT$5.83.
Free cash flow surged to NT$9.236 billion, while the net financial debt-to-EBITDA

ratio dropped from 59% in 4Q25 to 24% in 3Q26, reflecting a continuously
improving financial structure and robust cash generation.
Operational Outlook:
4Q26 revenue, GM, and OPM are all expected to edge up sequentially, defying
typical seasonal flat-to-down trends for the fourth quarter.
AI demand remains solid, with its contribution to total revenue expanding from
15% in 1Q26 and 16% in 2Q26 to approximately 20% in 3Q26.
Key Q&A:
Capacity & Expansion Plans: Capacity utilization rates stood at 95% in 3Q26 and

are expected to exceed 95% in 4Q26, surpassing the healthy range of 85%–90%.
Total capacity for tantalum capacitors and MLCCs is planned to expand by 10%–
15% in 2027, with portions coming online starting in the second quarter.
Order Momentum & B/B Ratio: The overall book-to-bill (B/B) ratio in 3Q26
remained close to 2.0, with AI-related tantalum capacitors and MLCCs seeing
particularly robust orders, while LTAs with core customers continue to underpin

future shipments.
Nan Ya Plastics (1303 TT)
Financial Highlights:

3Q26 revenue rose to NT$95.6bn (+14.3% QoQ), with electronic materials
expanding to 58.1% of total sales. Operating profit surged 61.2% QoQ to
NT$18.31bn, of which electronic materials contributed 93.8%.
EPS hit a record NT$4.90, bringing 9M26 EPS to a record NT$10.07, supported by
electronic material profitability and investment income from Nanya Technology.
Operational Outlook:
CCL has surpassed NYPCB as the segment's primary revenue driver (31% of

electronic materials) with segment OPM scaling to 30.9% (up from 19.9% in 2Q26).
Management guided for fiberglass fabrics and CCL price hikes to continue at a 5–
10% MoM increase pace through early 2027, alongside a notable product mix shift
toward M7/M8 in 1Q27.
Key Q&A:
CCL Pricing and M-Series Mix: CCL and fiberglass-cloth pricing remains firm into
early 2027 with monthly 5–10% increases. M-series products reached ~30% of
CCL revenue in September, with management expecting further mix improvement

toward M7/M8 as CSP projects lock in.
Capacity Expansion and M10 Development: Monthly CCL capacity is projected to
expand from 6.18m to 6.48m sheets by year-end. For M9/M10, Nan Ya is
independently formulating hydrocarbon resins and fiberglass incorporating low-
dielectric and quartz materials with solid sampling progress.
TUC (6274 TT)
Financial Highlights:
3Q26 financial results beat market consensus, reporting revenue of NT17.95bn,
GM of 36.9%, operating margin of 31.0%, and EPS of NT$12.88, outperforming

consensus estimates by 29% to 35%.
The earnings beat was driven by continuous growth in high-end product revenue
and a return to profitability for the Thailand plant, which successfully offset earlier
first-half losses.
Operational Outlook:
For 4Q26, high-end product pricing is initiating upward adjustments. With the
Thailand plant adding another 100k sheets of capacity and all series of CCL seeing
price hike opportunities (especially high-end M7/M8 catch-up price increases

alongside tightening mid-to-low-end fiberglass cloth supply), product mix and
profitability are set to improve further.
ASIC AI servers are emerging as a core growth engine, alongside long-term
opportunities from satellite and space data centers. TUC is adding several large
ASIC customers (some already in sampling/board qualification stages) and
expanding its customer base in 400G, 800G, and 1.6T switches.
Key Q&A / Detailed Highlights:
ASIC & Switch Expansion: AI servers will serve as the primary growth driver, with

multiple major ASIC clients entering the board-maker certification stage;
meanwhile, existing 400G switch customers are rapidly accelerating 800G and 1.6T
switch certifications, with some expecting 1.6T demand to outpace 800G by 2027.
Satellite and PTFE Solutions: Space satellite PCBs utilizing M8+ materials are slated
to begin contribution in 1H27, with long-term customer demand forecasts already
extended through 2032. For PTFE, TUC boasts ~5–6 years of R&D foundation

rather than being a newcomer, though external partnerships may be pursued to
bypass special high-temperature press machine capacity limitations

Win Semiconductors (3105 TT)
Financial Highlights:
3Q26 revenue reached NT$5.86bn (+11.5% QoQ / +31% YoY) with capacity
utilization improving to 75%. GM expanded significantly by 9.2ppts QoQ to an 18-
quarter high of 37.4% (standalone GM reached 40.7%), while OPM rose to 24.2%
and EPS reached NT$4.55.
Margin expansion was primarily driven by a favorable product mix shift toward
higher-margin Infrastructure and Optical products rather than utilization gains

alone.
Operational Outlook:
Management guided 4Q26 revenue for low-single-digit QoQ growth—beating
normal smartphone seasonality—with gross margins sustaining in the high-30%
range.
Moving into FY27, photodetector (PD) production is expected to ramp materially,
while CW Laser and EML are anticipated to contribute more meaningfully starting
in FY28[cite: 388].
Key Q&A:
Infrastructure and Satellite Growth: Infrastructure became WIN's largest revenue

contributor at 35–40% in 3Q26, driven by LEO satellites, aerospace, and long-
distance communications; demand is expected to stay robust into FY27.
Optical and PD Production Timeline: Optical revenue rose QoQ on initial PD mass
production; PD will see significantly higher full-year shipment volumes in FY27,
while CW Laser and EML will generate limited initial revenue before a FY28 volume
ramp.

Report Introduction
Our proprietary dbDataInsights survey shows cyber threats are evolving faster than organisations can adapt
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research.
“It’s the only thing that can shut down my business.”
That was the punchline from a conversation I had recently with the CEO of a large
privately held financial services company. This was his comment when I followed up

as to why cybersecurity risk is the number one concern that “keeps him up at night”.
To help understand this risk, our dbDataInsights team surveyed more than 50
cybersecurity experts. The uncomfortable conclusion is that many firms are
unprepared for cyber threats. Cyber risk is increasing as AI adoption accelerates.
—89% agree the threat landscape is evolving faster than organisations can adapt.
—78% say the gap between highly and poorly prepared organisations is widening.

What has changed with AI advancement? Cyberattacks have become faster,
cheaper, more deceptive and more scalable. Capabilities once reserved for
sophisticated actors are now broadly accessible to a new generation of profit-driven
criminals. At the same time, rising geopolitical tension has made cyber operations
an increasingly attractive low-cost, deniable tool for nation states. Corporates and
investors therefore need to understand their broader exposure and critical

infrastructure risk in their supply chain and with stakeholders.
Among other autonomous incidents, the recent Hugging Face incident
highlighted AI’s cyberattack potential. AI agents caused harm without
direction from a human attacker, with weak guardrails and no human
judgement to restrain them. Beyond the systemic questions it raised, the attack
highlights three additional concerns: (i) AI operates at a speed and scale humans
cannot match, with agents taking over 17,000+ actions in four days; (ii) the intrusion

began in the data-processing pipeline, where AI platforms are uniquely exposed;
and (iii) the agents escaped their test environment through a widely used third-party
tool representing supplier risk in an unfamiliar form.

### Page 4

Report summary and notable takeaways
Secular trends and AI adoption have widened the attack surface; the severity of losses is compounding
Source: FBI Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3), company security advisories, OECD, company reports, Deutsche Bank Research.
Secular trends have widened the attack surface. This includes rapid
digitalisation, widespread Internet of Things (IoT) endpoints, hybrid workforces,
cloud adoption and deeper supply chain dependencies. The scale of the threat

increase is reflected in data from the FBI Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3),
which shows cybercrime complaints more than doubled from 2019 pre-pandemic
baseline of ~467,000 to ~1.0mn in 2025, with a 3x expansion relative to 2017 levels.
Barriers to entry are lower; attack tools are now sold “off the shelf”. Today,
cybercrime marketplaces, available on the dark web, look less like the underworld
portrayed in films and more like an online software store. Buyers can access AI-

powered tools that scan for software vulnerabilities (see top-right chart showing
reported CVEs after the release of cyber capable frontier models), write malicious
code at speed, and provide access to malware that rewrites its own code to avoid
detection as it runs. Ransomware-as-a-service (RaaS) takes it further, renting out
ready-made attack kits, often in exchange for a portion of the profits.
Impersonation is becoming harder to detect. Hackers can now generate more

realistic phishing messages, while voice cloning and deepfakes make impersonation
convincing enough to gain access unnoticed. With stolen credentials, attackers often
aren’t hacking in, they are simply logging in.
The rise of loose cybercriminal collectives. Some of the most damaging recent
attacks have been linked to loosely organised, often English-speaking networks such
as ShinyHunters and Scattered Spider. The latter has been widely named as the

group behind the 2025 Marks & Spencer cyberattack, in which the hacking group
deployed RaaS. AI is enabling these groups to become larger and faster, widening
the gap between the speed of attack and a defender’s response.
The severity of losses is compounding. A 2026 IBM study puts the average breach
at a record USD ~5.0mn, up 12% YoY, but the impact on large companies can run
into hundreds of millions or even billions. Estimates for global cyber insurance

premiums (per Munich Re) point to a rise from roughly USD 7.0bn in 2020 to USD
15.0bn in 2025, with expectations to reach USD 28.0bn by 2030.

### Page 5

5
Among the more concerning survey takeaways is that a large-scale and
significant systemic incident is now the base case.
— 83% expect a significant systemic attack within two years and 81% expect it to be
moderately, very, or extremely severe for economic activity and markets.
— 81% say AI favours attackers today, with not one respondent putting defenders
ahead of attackers. Only 20% expect defenders to be ahead in five years.
Geopolitical tensions coupled with rising cyber capabilities have transformed

cyberspace into a digital battlefield between global powers. Intent has moved
towards espionage and disruption of not only critical infrastructure (e.g., healthcare,
finance, power, industrial systems, defence) but private companies as well. Chinese
President Xi Jinping has linked cybersecurity to national security and economic and
social stability, with China’s public statements similarly emphasising the protection
of critical infrastructure. At the same time, the US’s June 2025 Executive Order on

Cybersecurity reflects its continued focus on national cyber resilience.
Europe relies on US cyber tools while its start-ups lag in funding. Reliance on US
security vendors exposes European firms to potential access, update or export
restrictions over the long-term. Shrinking this gap will prove difficult given US cyber
start-ups attract nearly 6x the venture and growth funding of European peers. And
while the timing of quantum computing (which could break widely used encryption

methods) remains uncertain, it could further deepen Europe’s dependency.
Ultimately, cyber preparedness is a financial variable. Investors should ask what
firms spend on security versus AI adoption, how well they know their vulnerabilities,
and how fast they can recover.
— Only 24% of respondents think organisations have sufficient visibility into the
threats relevant to them, even though 57% saw cybersecurity budgets rise over
the past 12 months.

— 81% agree that cybersecurity related laws and regulation cannot keep pace, and
65% doubt leadership understands cyber risk well enough.

### Page 6

6
When cyber weakness = share price consequence…
Investors are more aware of cyber risk today, but they rarely sell a stock due to
perceived cyber vulnerabilities (although we are aware of some who have).
—What is the potential impact? Our dbDataInsights event study of 47 breaches
shows a cumulative breach-attributed return of c. -2.9% within the first two
trading days and c. -6.0% through first 20 days trading days. The erosion then
continues more gradually, reaching c. -6.7% nine months after disclosure (see

slide 13).
Which industries are most vulnerable?
—Our survey respondents point to (1) government & public sector (52%), (2)
financial services (50%), (3) energy and utilities (46%) and (4) healthcare and life
sciences follow (39%, rising to 48% among US respondents).
We should caveat, that high sector exposure does not mean every company in it is
equally vulnerable. Some firms in these “exposed” sectors may very well be better
prepared given they have built specialist teams and defence protocols for decades.

What about access to credit and ratings? Credit and rating providers are paying
attention. Moody’s and S&P both cut their outlook on Jaguar Land Rover within two
months of its 2025 hack. Today, this type of impact is not widespread. But if risk
escalates through a systemic incident or an AI-driven step change in attacks these
mechanisms, including ESG ratings, could reprice asset ownership or impact access
to credit. In fact, some lenders are already pricing it. Journal of Corporate Finance

research using US syndicated loan data found borrowers with persistently higher cyber
risk pay 4–13bp more.
The bottom line? While a broader systemic attack is hard to prepare for, corporates
and investors should be asking questions about vulnerabilities, security investment,
preparedness and recovery should an attack occur. This is especially true for
industries quickly adopting AI capabilities and / or a high threat landscape.

dbDataInsights Cybersecurity Expert Survey methodology
—This survey was conducted among 54 cybersecurity professionals across the US
and Europe in September of 2026. Questions took the form of either multiple

choice selections or free form responses. The dbDataInsights team received the
responses anonymously, but respondents were required to work or consult in
cyber security roles
—Who took the survey? On self-identified roles, around 80% sit inside an
organisation’s cybersecurity function, with the remainder working independently
(15%) or in academia and research (6%). The most common titles were security
architect (23%), Chief Information Security Officer (16%) and security analyst (9%),

alongside Head of Security, Deputy CISO, Incident Response Lead and Security
Operations Lead.
—Only a small minority of respondents work for cyber resellers, thus survey views
are primarily attributable to those working within an organization on
cybersecurity.
—Survey participants were also asked for their perspectives on cyber resilience and
governance, what is misunderstood, alongside emerging threats, risk to AI
adoption and the influence of geopolitics.

—While responses were anonymous, in some cases we conducted follow up
interviews with certain participants where contact details were made available.
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research.

### Page 10

10
Risk from AI-enabled attacks and added AI adoption vulnerabilities
Insider threats and supply chain compromises remain a growing concern
“The fear of AI writing undetectable malware is … overblown. Attackers were
never short of malware, since they can buy it ready made along with initial
access. Good detection looks at behaviour rather than exact code, so a rewritten
payload still has to do the same suspicious things.”
“Frontier AI models are currently the main hype. Automated phishing and

scams are underappreciated, yet they are responsible for the bigger
monetary losses at the moment.”
“There are too many organisations relying on the same providers for
critical services and this means single points of failure.”
“A corporation might be very secure but if they rely on a third party that
does not have the same level of security posture, [it] could be
compromised via this third-party (basically their weak link).”
“Existing employee login behaviour and usage of AI is underappreciated –

it’s what poses the biggest threat to a breach.”
“No company can guarantee they won’t be breached, so there needs to
be more focus put on the ability to recover and survive an attack.”
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research .
